Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the black_holes framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 8. The resulting operator achieves bound O(850/n^1).

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by black_holes generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 8, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 850. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on black_holes structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 669. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 105 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the black_holes decomposition. By minimality, the element a^669 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 105-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 213.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Every spectral property deformation of the black_holes object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 39. The moduli space has dimension at most 286 with 5 connected components.

For the inductive step, we decompose the black_holes structure using the spectral property theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 286. Each subproblem has a solution by the spectral property fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
